## Status icons — the canonical definitions

These six are the ENTIRE vocabulary. `scripts/uat-audit.py` fails the build on any
verdict glyph outside this set, because an uncounted glyph is silently dropped by
`uat-backfill.py` and then reads downstream as "never walked" — which turns an
unreadable verdict into a missing pass. Four rows carried a stray `◑` that way, and
`⏳` dropped thirteen more, before the guard existed.

| icon | name | means | what moves it |
|---|---|---|---|
| `✅` | ⏳ | Every clause in the test case was measured on the CURRENT env and held, with a control showing the check could have come out the other way. | Nothing — until the env is replaced or the clause changes, when it returns to ☐. |
| `❌` | FAIL | Measured on the current env and it did NOT hold. A named, reproducible defect. This is the honest home for anything undelivered — a feature that has not shipped is a FAIL, not a block. | A fix, deployed, then re-walked. |
| `⚠️` | PARTIAL | **Working state only — never a terminal verdict.** Some clauses proven, others not. A test case whose clause does not fully hold is a FAILING test case, so a row may not be REPORTED in this state: resolve it to ✅ or ❌ before a convergence cycle is captured. It exists so a walk-in-progress can be saved mid-flight. | Closing the named residual (→ ✅) or accepting it as the failure it is (→ ❌). |
| `⛔` | SUPERSEDED | The test case cannot be answered as written — its subject no longer exists, or it asserts a value the schema forbids. **Not** a parking space for hard work. | Retire-and-REPLACE, one-for-one, recorded in `uat-retirements.csv`. |
| `☐` | NOTRUN | Never walked on this env. Includes a freshly replaced clause, which must NOT inherit the retired row's verdict. | Walking it. |
| `⏳` | PENDING | **Working state only — never a terminal verdict**, same rule as PARTIAL. Written by `scripts/reset-uat.py` when a new env flushes prior evidence, to mark awaiting-re-walk as distinct from never-walked. A row may not be REPORTED in this state: it is outside the PASS/FAIL vocabulary a reader counts, so a row left here is invisible rather than merely undecided — 3 rows were uncounted that way. | Re-walking on the live env, then ✅ or ❌. |

**The denominator is STONE at 286.** Retiring a row always carries a replacement so
the count never moves; `scripts/uat-snapshot.py` refuses any other total. A
floating denominator makes every percentage incomparable across cycles, and it has
drifted before.

**Evidence is per-env.** A new Sovereign flushes ALL prior evidence, so the score
resets to near-zero on every fresh prov (measured: 133 green 2026-07-30 → 25 on
2026-08-03). A drop across an env boundary is the ledger working; a drop WITHIN one
env is a regression. `scripts/uat-drift-guard.py` refuses a ✅ row that cites a
wiped env.

THE CONTROL, and it is what makes this more than a link-MTU screenshot: a PMTU/DF black hole is SIZE-DEPENDENT — the handshake and small responses succeed and only >MSS bulk stalls. THE CONTROL: the identical request with a deliberately wrong secret returned `unauthorized_client / "Invalid client or Invalid client credentials"`. An anonymous browser at https://console.<slug> is served the single catalyst-ui door which immediately runs the per-Org silent OIDC check — console.acme-corp.omani.homes -> HTTP 200, final URL https://auth.acme-corp.omani.homes/realms/sovereign/protocol/openid-connect/auth?response_type=code&client_id=catalyst-ui&redirect_uri=https%3A%2F%2Fconsole.acme-corp.omani.homes%2Fauth%2Fcallback&scope=openid+profile+email&state=…&code_challenge_method=S256&prompt=none; console.walk-two.omani.rest and console.delta-corp.omani.homes reproduce identically on their own auth hosts. WHY PARTIAL, two live gaps: (a) the door only answers ~50% of the time — pinned to 212.72.24.82, console.acme-corp 5/10 200, console.delta-corp 4/10, console.walk-two 4/10, rest ERR_CONNECTION_RESET, while console.hw290.omani.works on the SAME IP is 10/10 200 and raw TCP 212.72.24.82:443 is 10/10 OK (delta-corp needed an in-run retry before it loaded); SNI-selective failure of the *.<orgslug> wildcard listeners on Gateway kube-system/cilium-gateway-console. (b) console.gamma-corp.omani.homes does not resolve at all (NXDOMAIN) and has no gateway listener — that Organization has no console door whatsoever (it is also the CR-less Org: organizations.orgs.openova.io/gamma-corp NotFound). ‖ —  ‖ 2026-08-07 SOURCE ADJUDICATION of gap (a), and it REFUTES the hypothesis this row's own evidence invites. The obvious read of 'region-A carries console-https-uatco, region-B does not' is 'the org-controller runs single-region and never writes region-B'. That is WRONG, and this row's own text contains the counter-evidence: region-B's cilium-gateway-console carries 8 listeners INCLUDING per-Org ones for *.r17probe.omani.homes. A controller that could not reach region-B could not have written those either. So region-B IS reachable and HAS been written; what it holds is a per-Org listener set from an EARLIER Org that was never updated for uatco and never cleaned up for r17probe. Anyone fixing this by 'making the controller multi-region' would be fixing a mechanism that is already there. WHAT THE SOURCE SHOWS. tenant_console_tls.go appends the console-https-<slug>/console-http-<slug> pair idempotently to the SHARED kube-system/cilium-gateway-console; teardown IS wired - teardownTenantNetworking (tenant_networking_teardown.go:112) is called from organization_controller.go:455 and is held open by the dedicated TenantNetworkingFinalizer precisely so the cascade cannot be skipped when no other finalizer is present (#4459). So neither the write nor the delete path is missing code. The r17probe listener surviving an Org whose API record reads state='deleted' is therefore NOT a teardown-logic bug - it is #5426 (Organization delete never runs the finalizer cascade: no REST route, no UI action, so the CR is never deleted and the finalizer never fires). The listener outlives the Org because nothing ever asked it to die. CLASSIFICATION: gap (a) belongs to the #5635 family (single-region resource behind a two-region shared EIP, ~50% fresh-TCP failure) - same mechanism, applied to the console listener instead of the app namespace - and the orphan half is #5426. Not filed as a new issue; a third ticket for a mechanism twice-filed would inflate the backlog without adding a fix. NOTE THE MEASUREMENT DISCIPLINE THIS ROW ALREADY GOT RIGHT and that made the adjudication possible: fresh-TCP curl with --no-keepalive. Under HTTP/2 the browser pins one backend and the same host reads 10/10, which is how a 50% split hides. ‖ hw292-2026-08-10 PLATFORM REGRESSION, DATED. Same dated cause as row 87 (#5246). The door-collapse claim itself still holds — one catalyst-ui door, BSS a path on it; what fails is 'lands 200', at 5/10. ‖ NO-PARTIALS DISPOSITION 2026-08-10 — RECLASSIFIED ⚠️ -> ❌. Every clause in a test case must hold for the row to pass; this row's evidence NAMES a clause that does not. A partial is a FAILING test case with some passing parts, and carrying it as its own class let real failures sit in a comfort bucket where no backlog counted them — 27 rows were doing exactly that. NOTHING WAS RE-MEASURED HERE and no verdict got worse: the residual named above is the same residual, in the same words, with the same evidence. Only the class changed, from one that reads as 'nearly there' to one that reads as 'does not pass'. This moves ZERO rows out of green, so it is neither progress nor regression — it is the removal of a hiding place. The row closes when the named residual closes. ‖ PARTITION 2026-08-10: ENV-STATE — needs an environment SHAPE this Sovereign does not have (pre-cutover window, single region, or a destructive prov->wipe cycle). Not a code gap and not re-walkable here. ‖ RE-TAGGED 2026-08-10: NEEDS-CODE -> DEPLOY-GATED. The fix for this row is now MERGED on main as 0439fbea8 (PR #5957). It is not running on hw292 — the deployed images pre-date it — so the verdict correctly stays ❌ and a re-walk here would reproduce it. What changed is the CLASS: this row no longer waits on engineering, it waits on a roll or a fresh prov. The merge SHA is the on-disk artifact. ‖ CITATION CORRECTED 2026-08-10: the SHA in the re-tag above read 83f2a5521, which is the docs-only LEDGER commit itself (#5962 — docs/ledger/UAT.md + WBS-TO-100.md + uat-raw.csv and nothing else); the same SHA was pasted onto ELEVEN rows under three different PR numbers, so anyone who checked the warrant would have found a prose diff and concluded the whole re-tag was invented. Verdict and partition are unchanged — only the pointer was wrong. Now enforced pre-merge by scripts/check-uat-fix-sha-citations.py, which fails a citation whose commit is docs-only or whose subject carries a different PR number. ‖ PARTITION CONTRADICTION RESOLVED 2026-08-10 — this cell carried TWO partition labels at once: "PARTITION: ENV-STATE" and, after it, "RE-TAGGED: NEEDS-CODE -> DEPLOY-GATED", which also names a class the row was never in. DEPLOY-GATED is the correct one and ENV-STATE is refuted on the row's own evidence: the residual is not an environment SHAPE this Sovereign lacks, it is that the per-Org console listener pair is written to region A only while the console EIP's backend pool spans both regions, so roughly half of fresh TCP connections reach a region with no listener for that SNI and are reset. That is a code defect with a merged fix — 0439fbea8 (PR #5957) makes every write in tenant_console_tls.go fan over consoleRegionTargets, built from the cutover-secondary-kubeconfigs the #5359 bridge already materializes, and carries the teardown and the #5395 postcondition verifier over the same set. hw292 needs no different shape; it needs that image. ‖ RE-TAGGED 2026-08-11: DEPLOY-GATED -> NEEDS-CODE — the cited fix 0439fbea8 (PR #5957) IS in the artifact this env runs. The organization-controller image is 4d1da63 and 0439fbea8 is an ancestor of it, so the row's own warrant — that the deployed images pre-date the fix — is false here. Measured read-only against BOTH region kubeconfigs on 2026-08-11: region A kube-system/cilium-gateway-console carries console-https-hw293walkone and console-https-hw293walktwo, region B carries ONLY the three Sovereign hostname pairs and ZERO per-Org listeners. That is the exact state #5957 exists to prevent, reproduced on top of #5957. ROOT CAUSE READ AT THE EDIT SITE rather than inferred: tenant_console_tls_regions.go builds its region set from the #5359 bridge Secret catalyst/cutover-secondary-kubeconfigs and cross-checks it against kube-system/cilium-clustermesh as an independent witness; on this env NEITHER object exists — clustermesh is established (clustermesh-apiserver 1/1) but publishes clustermesh-apiserver-*-cert instead, and the cutover chart is still installing per #6004 — so the witness declares zero remote regions and the file's own documented single-region path takes over. Its comment says a Sovereign with no ClusterMesh Secret 'behaves EXACTLY as before this file existed', which is what a 2-region Sovereign is now doing. The fix is deployed and inert, so what is owed is engineering, not a roll. |

| G10 | placement | [#3969](https://github.com/openova-io/openova/issues/3969) | Placement EPIC acceptance: EVERY Application CR carries a non-empty `spec.placement` drawn from the canonical set (singleton / active-passive / active-hot-standby) — the count of placed apps equals the count of Application CRs, with no app left unplaced. | hw293-2026-08-10 | ✅ | hw293-2026-08-10 READ-ONLY LIVE COUNT — PASS, and the vacuity the previous ❌ was graded on is gone. `kubectl get applications.apps.openova.io -A` now returns **SEVEN** Application CRs across the two Organizations, and **every one carries a non-empty `spec.placement` whose mode is in the canonical set — 6 singleton + 1 active-hot-standby, 0 unplaced**: hw293walkone/uat174-wp (singleton), hw293walkone/uat50-ahs-pg (active-hot-standby, regions me-east-215-a + -b), hw293walkone/uat224-openclaw (singleton), hw293walktwo/uat174-fail-pg (singleton), hw293walktwo/uat232-openclaw (singleton), hw293walktwo/uat59-single-pg (singleton), hw293walktwo/uatm4-agenity (singleton). The clause's arithmetic — "the count of placed apps equals the count of Application CRs, with no app left unplaced" — is 7 == 7 over a NON-EMPTY population, which is what the old 0 == 0 could not be. NON-VACUITY, stated as mechanism rather than asserted: the CRD carries `x-kubernetes-preserve-unknown-fields` on `placement` with NO schema default and `spec.required` is only `[blueprintRef]`, so an Application CR with no placement at all is schema-valid and would have been counted unplaced by the same census (the counter prints `unplaced: N` from the same pass, and this row's own prior stamp is the record of that census returning zero objects on this very cluster). SHAPE DIVERGENCE WORTH NAMING, since it is not cosmetic: 3 of the 7 carry placement as an OBJECT (`{mode, regions}`) and 4 as a bare STRING (`"singleton"`) — both forms are accepted by the CRD and both express a canonical mode, so the clause holds either way, but the two shapes are consumed differently downstream (see row 63, where the object-form active-hot-standby app still renders `Pattern: singleton` because the console prefers `status.perCluster` over the declared placement). SCOPE: region B does not serve `applications.apps.openova.io` at all (CRD NotFound), so the 7 in region A are the whole population, not a per-region slice. |

Ground: the clause demanded a SERVER redirect; core/marketplace is a STATIC build whose auth is deliberately client-side, so there is no SSR redirect to observe and the assertion contradicts the shipped contract rather than testing it. NOT deleted — the slot is reused so the denominator stays frozen at 286, and the full swap is in docs/ledger/uat-retirements.csv. Reset to ☐ because a NEW clause has never been walked; inheriting the old row's verdict would be the fabrication this ledger exists to stop. ‖ hw292-2026-08-10 WALKED ON THE REPLACEMENT CLAUSE - FAILS, and the mechanism is architectural rather than a bug in the page. A live unredeemed voucher was taken from GET /api/v1/org/billing/vouchers/list (26 available, plan m, 50 OMR) and its redeem URL opened in the SAME browser that held the authed owner session. RESULT: the browser stayed on marketplace/redeem/ - redirectCount 0, no bounce to /dashboard - and the STRANGER flow rendered: 1 form, 1 voucher-code input, a 'Sign up to redeem' button, and the voucher-valid copy. The owner identity appears nowhere on the page. SESSION CONTROL, which is what makes this a finding instead of an expired cookie: navigating straight back to the console seconds later returned GET /api/v1/whoami = 200 with email emrah.baysal@openova.io and tier `owner`. The session was live the whole time. MECHANISM, measured not argued: document.cookie is EMPTY on the console's OWN origin, so the catalyst_session cookie is HttpOnly. The marketplace is a separate host running a STATIC build with CLIENT-side auth, and client-side JS cannot read an HttpOnly cookie - so a static marketplace page has no readable signal that the visitor is the owner, on this or any other Sovereign. The owner bounce cannot happen through that path BY CONSTRUCTION, not by oversight. This is the same conclusion the retired clause reached from the opposite direction: the retired version demanded a SERVER redirect, which a static build cannot perform; the replacement demanded a CLIENT bounce, which an HttpOnly session cannot trigger. Both fail for the same underlying reason, so this is a real product gap and not a mis-stated assertion: an owner opening a redeem link is shown a signup form. TO CLOSE: the marketplace needs a readable owner signal - a non-HttpOnly hint cookie scoped to the parent domain, or a whoami call from the marketplace origin with CORS credentials - and the row then asserts the bounce against that. ‖ PARTITION 2026-08-10: NEEDS-CODE — no fix exists yet. This row is engineering work, not a walk; a re-walk cannot change it. ‖ RE-ADJUDICATED 2026-08-10 — NO FURTHER ACTION, LEFT RED. This slot was already retired-and-replaced today AND then walked on the replacement clause, which failed for a MEASURED architectural reason (an HttpOnly session cookie plus a static marketplace origin, with a live whoami=200 as the session control). A second rewrite would be re-wording a clause that has already been re-worded once and then tested; the residual is product work, not ledger work. ‖ RE-TAGGED 2026-08-11: NEEDS-CODE -> WALKABLE NOW — the fix EXISTS, is purpose-built for this row, and is SERVED on this env. #5965 (`f25c4d7dc`, 2026-08-10T09:32Z) added `products/catalyst/bootstrap/api/internal/handler/session_hint.go`: a non-HttpOnly `catalyst_session_hint` companion set alongside every `catalyst_session` and scoped to the same `sessionCookieDomain` — literally the readable owner signal this cell's own TO-CLOSE line asks for, and its file header names UAT row 3. Consumed client-side by `core/marketplace/src/lib/sessionHint.ts`, `redeemDestination.ts` and `returningVisitor.ts`, with `redeem.astro` checking the hint BEFORE the server probe. RENDERED-ARTIFACT PROOF rather than a source grep, because this storefront is a static build: `GET https://marketplace.hw293.omantel.biz/redeem/` returns 200 and the SERVED HTML contains `catalyst_session_hint` three times. Merged AND deployed AND never re-walked since, so a walk on THIS env is what decides it. |

| 25 | model | [#3687](https://github.com/openova-io/openova/issues/3687) | One consistent model across surfaces: `/organizations` shows the Orgs that `/apps`, the dashboard and showback all agree on. | hw292-2026-08-10 | ❌ | hw292-2026-08-06T01:55Z RE-WALK (dep 1c56518035a83e03, authed owner, four surfaces sampled in one pass): IMPROVED on one residual, two persist, two new. RESOLVED - hw291 residual (c): the apps feed and the CRs now agree exactly, 14 == 14 (see row 19), so the installed-vs-CR gap is closed. PERSISTS - residual (a) in altered form: GET /api/v1/org/consumption labels the parent group 'hw292.omani.works' (isParent:true, costUnits 0) while the directory and the Organization CR both say 'hw292-omani-works' - the literal word 'sovereign' is gone but showback and the directory still do not use the same identifier for the same Org. PERSISTS - residual (b) partially: the treemap Organization layer renders 'Platform overhead' + 'uatco' but the PARENT Org hw292-omani-works has no node, while showback does emit it (at 0 units). NEW (i): the /apps grid agrees with neither - it shows 0 of the 14 Applications and 46 blueprint slots instead, so 'the Orgs that /apps agrees on' cannot be evaluated from that surface at all. NEW (ii): GET /api/v1/organizations returns 3 records where only 2 Organization CRs exist - the extra is r17probe with state:'deleted' and no CR, and /organizations still renders a link to it. Soft-deleted rather than the #5395 orphan class, but the directory and the CR set still do not match 1:1.  ‖ RESIDUAL (a) FIXED IN SOURCE 2026-08-07 (#5819, PR pending; deploy-gated on hw292), AND IT ALSO EXPLAINS RESIDUAL (b). consumptionParentOrg returned the RAW Sovereign FQDN, so showback labelled the parent group 'hw292.omani.works' while the Organization CR, the directory and the treemap all use the slug form 'hw292-omani-works' (spineOrganizationSlug -> orgNamespace). Two spellings of one Org is exactly what this row asserts against. Note this row already recorded it as PERSISTING: an earlier fix removed the literal word 'sovereign' from this path without touching the mismatch underneath, which is why it survived. IT WAS NEVER ONLY COSMETIC. The parent bucket is selected by `org == parentOrg`, where org comes from the openova.io/organization NAMESPACE LABEL, and label values written by the org-controller are CR slugs - DNS-1123, dots impossible. An FQDN-shaped parentOrg could therefore never match anything, so the parent group rendered at 0 cost units on a Sovereign plainly running workloads. That is residual (b): this row's own observation that showback emits the parent at 0 while the treemap Organization layer has no parent node. ONE root cause, TWO symptoms - they were recorded as separate residuals. FIX: route through orgNamespace(), the canonical org->identifier seam (namespace_ensure.go), the same one the self-org CR producer uses. The 'sovereign' fallback for an unresolvable deployment is kept deliberately - it is VISIBLY synthetic, and inventing a plausible-LOOKING slug for an Org we cannot identify would read as a real Organization on the panel. 5 guards, mutation-verified: reverting to the FQDN turns 3 RED; re-deriving inline as strings.ReplaceAll(fqdn,'.','-') turns 2 RED; deleting the reasoning comment turns 1 RED. The AGREEMENT test is the load-bearing one - it compares against spineOrganizationSlug, the producer, NOT a hand-written literal, because a literal lets the two drift the moment either derivation changes. The SEAM test is why the inline mutation exists: ReplaceAll gets hw292.omani.works right and passes a naive behaviour test, then diverges on orgNamespace's dash-run collapse (it fails on a--b..c.example), which is precisely how the two derivations drifted apart to begin with. Gates: go vet clean, full handler suite 255s green. NOT CLAIMED: residual NEW (i) - the /apps grid showing 0 of 14 Applications - is #5814. Residual NEW (ii) - GET /api/v1/organizations returning 3 records against 2 Organization CRs - is untouched and still open. ‖ RESIDUAL (ii) FIXED IN SOURCE 2026-08-07 (#5833, PR pending; deploy-gated on hw292). '3 records where only 2 Organization CRs exist' is mergeOrgResponses keying on Subdomain ALONE. Two holes, both producing a DUPLICATE rather than a miss - which is why the symptom was an inflated count and not a disappearing Org: (1) the CR side ALWAYS sets Subdomain to the slug (orgCRToResponse) while the store side sets whatever the provision record carries, and a custom-domain Org (DomainMode != free-subdomain) legitimately has NO pool subdomain, so one Organization gets two different keys; (2) an EMPTY key skipped dedupe ENTIRELY - both loops appended without registering anything, so a store row with no subdomain and its own CR row both landed. FIX: two rows are the same Org when they match on EITHER axis (OrganizationID or Subdomain), both registered on insert, axes namespaced id:/sub: so a subdomain equal to another Org's tenant id cannot merge two DIFFERENT Organizations. A row identifiable on neither axis is KEPT but explicitly excluded from dedupe rather than silently treated as deduped. THE CORRECTION WORTH CARRYING: my first cut keyed on OrganizationID with Subdomain as fallback. ALL SIX TARGETED TESTS PASSED. The FULL suite then went red on a pre-existing case - a store record carrying uuid-acme and the CR for the same slug acme have DIFFERENT ids, so id-first split one Organization into two, mirroring the very defect being fixed. Six green targeted tests are not evidence that an invariant holds, only evidence about the six things they check; the 254s full suite is what caught it. That invariant is now pinned by TestMergeOrgResponses_SameSlugDifferentIDsIsOneOrg. 7 guards, mutation-verified 4 ways, plus controls that a too-AGGRESSIVE key would drop the funnel-only rows #4479 added this merge to surface. Gates: go vet clean, full handler suite 254s green. STILL OPEN ON THIS ROW: residual (a) identifier mismatch is #5819 (merged), NEW (i) /apps showing 0 of 14 Applications is #5814 (merged) - both deploy-gated; the treemap parent-node half rides on #5819's join repair. ‖ hw292-2026-08-10 VERDICT PROMOTED FROM ⚠️ — deploy-gate CONFIRMED by two-way ancestry. The cited fix is verifiably NOT an ancestor of the deployed image, and the CONTROL runs the identical command against a commit that IS one (`<deployed>^` → ancestor, exit 0) and one that is not (origin/main HEAD → exit 1), so the check has been seen to answer both ways. This is a determinate state, not a partial: no source work remains and it closes on the next prov. hw292 cannot receive it — the cutover chart wants 0.1.174, has 0.1.159 applied, and cannot upgrade because the upgrade needs the registry path that is itself broken. Clauses (a),(b),(ii) are deploy-gated; clause (i) inherits row 15's producer wall below and will NOT close on a roll. ‖ NO-PARTIALS DISPOSITION 2026-08-10 — RECLASSIFIED ⚠️ -> ❌. Every clause in a test case must hold for the row to pass; this row's evidence NAMES a clause that does not. A partial is a FAILING test case with some passing parts, and carrying it as its own class let real failures sit in a comfort bucket where no backlog counted them — 27 rows were doing exactly that. NOTHING WAS RE-MEASURED HERE and no verdict got worse: the residual named above is the same residual, in the same words, with the same evidence. Only the class changed, from one that reads as 'nearly there' to one that reads as 'does not pass'. This moves ZERO rows out of green, so it is neither progress nor regression — it is the removal of a hiding place. The row closes when the named residual closes. ‖ PARTITION 2026-08-10: DEPLOY-GATED — the fix is MERGED and simply not running on this env. Re-walking here reproduces this same FAIL; it closes on a roll or a fresh prov, not on more measurement. ‖ RE-TAGGED 2026-08-11: DEPLOY-GATED -> WALKABLE NOW — the fix this row waits on is already in the artifact, so no roll changes it. The cross-surface consistency fixes cited against this row are ancestors of the running catalyst-api and catalyst-ui 70f6b07, and the row carries no hw293 evidence at all. Four surfaces on one authed pass is a walk, not a roll. |

The precondition the 2026-08-10T14:08Z walk could not find NOW EXISTS: hw293walkone/uat50-ahs-pg declares `spec.placement.mode: active-hot-standby` with `standbyRegions [hw-me-east-215-b-rtz-prod]`, and `continuums.dr.openova.io` + `cnpgpairs.dr.openova.io` are BOTH empty cluster-wide — precisely this row's declares-AHS-with-no-Continuum-backing case. THE HONEST NO-BACKING DR SECTION STILL DOES NOT RENDER: on /app/uat50-ahs-pg/topology the page paints, but there is NO element whose data-testid starts `topology-tab-dr` at all — no `topology-tab-dr-switchover`, no `topology-tab-dr-switchover-open` button, no "Switchover unavailable" reason text. So the clause fails on its FIRST TWO parts (copy present, control present-and-disabled); the third holds only by absence, which is the very confusion this row exists to forbid — an absent control and a disabled one are not the same statement. MECHANISM, read off the page's own network calls and the deployed component: `GET /applications/uat50-ahs-pg/placement?namespace=hw293walkone` returns **`{"targets":[],"derivedFromRuntime":true}`** — no runtime targets — so TopologyTab falls through to its next source, `status.perCluster`, which holds exactly ONE entry `{cluster: rtz-A, role: "singleton"}` because the application-controller fanned the HelmRelease out to region A only ("installed across 1 region(s)"). That single entry is mapped to one Primary target and RETURNS EARLY, so the chain never reaches `status.targets` — which on the very same object DOES carry the right pair, `[{region a, role Primary},{region b, role Standby, standbyType Hot}]`. Result: `hasStandby = targets.some(role==='Standby')` is false; `/continuum/dr-uat50-ahs-pg/replication-status` answers `source: "pending"` with healthGate `live-status Warn "awaiting region-b convergence"`, so `hasLiveDR` is false; `showDR = hasStandby \|\| hasLiveDR` is false and the whole DR block is skipped. THE PAGE CONTRADICTS ITSELF IN TWO PLACES, which is why this is a defect and not just a missing region: the Placement panel prints "Pattern: singleton · declared · not observed" for an app whose CR says active-hot-standby, while the Status panel two panels below prints "hw-me-east-215-b-rtz-prod Standby·Hot is reconciling" and the hero lists both region chips. RE-MEASURED ON TWO INDEPENDENTLY CREATED HEALTHY CONTEXTS (the second needed 4 fresh contexts before one got a live socket — the region-B split), byte-identical result both times, so this is not a transport artifact. SCREENSHOT: [📷 row63-ahs-no-dr-section-hw293.png](../../docs/sessions/2026-08-10/evidence/row63-ahs-no-dr-section-hw293.png) (hw293-2026-08-10). |

| 67 | topology | [#3375](https://github.com/openova-io/openova/issues/3375) | grafana status/overview reports Healthy/Running in both regions — no "cannot resolve write host" crashloop in the app health panel. | hw293-2026-08-10 | ❌ | hw292-2026-08-06T01:54Z RE-WALK (dep 1c56518035a83e03, authed owner, BOTH region kubeconfigs looped): PARTIAL, with the console half materially better than hw291 but the stated residual still literally present. HEALTH HALF PASSES and is sampled per region, not once: grafana pods are 3/3 Running in region-a AND region-b, and a grep over the last 300 lines of all containers returns 'cannot resolve write host' count 0 in region-a and 0 in region-b - no crashloop. Live rollout status is tracked via Flux.' - which is exactly the hw291 residual, unchanged. Cause is structural and unfixed on main: bootstrap-kit HRs have no Application CR, so GET .../applications/bp-grafana/status returns 404 application-not-found and there is no per-region health to render. Category (a): no merged-on-main fix found for this leg.  ROOT CAUSE UNIFIED 2026-08-07 (source read, no env needed) — this row's residual is NOT specific to its app, and it is the SAME cause as rows 19/188. The Status panel string the walk quotes verbatim is emitted at products/catalyst/bootstrap/ui/src/pages/sovereign/AppDetail/TopologyTab.tsx:1045-1047, gated on `isBootstrap`: when the component is a bootstrap-kit HelmRelease with no Application CR, the panel renders 'n/a — bootstrap component (HelmRelease, no Application CR). Live rollout status is tracked via Flux.' unconditionally. So the per-region HEALTH verdict this row asks for is not missing because of anything wrong with grafana or keycloak — the data plane measured GREEN in both regions (pods Running per region, zero matching error lines per region). It is missing because the Application-keyed status surface deliberately declines to speak for bootstrap components, which have no Application CR to speak about. ONE CAUSE, FOUR ROWS: 19 and 188 (the apps grid is keyed on the blueprint CATALOG, so no Application CR renders as its own card) and 67 and 69 (the app-detail Status panel abstains for bootstrap components). Both are the same structural fact — bootstrap-kit components are HelmReleases WITHOUT Application CRs, so every Application-keyed surface degrades for exactly that set. Fixing one without the other would move a symptom. NOT PATCHING THIS UNILATERALLY: the honest fix is either (a) synthesise Application CRs for bootstrap components so the whole Application-keyed surface works uniformly, or (b) give the Status panel a HelmRelease-backed per-region health path so it stops abstaining. (a) changes the object model; (b) changes what 'Status' means. Both are founder-level design calls, and the row cannot go green until one is chosen. ‖ THE SINGLE REMAINING RESIDUAL IS FIXED IN SOURCE 2026-08-07 (#5835, PR pending; deploy-gated on hw292), and it was the SAME defect in rows 67 and 69. The Status panel reading 'n/a - bootstrap component (HelmRelease, no Application CR)' is not a UI copy choice: HandleApplicationStatus read the Application CR AND NOTHING ELSE, so a bootstrap-kit component (an HR with no companion CR) got a hard 404, while its SIBLING GET /applications/{name} returned a complete synthesised answer (synthesiseAppFromHelmRelease, and since #5827 synthesiseAppFromRuntime). The console reacted the only way it could - TopologyTab.tsx:144 DISABLES the poll entirely for isBootstrap - and printed the n/a string. So one page contradicted itself: this row's own walk measured the header and Overview reading STATUS Ready, from the synthesised detail response, while the Status panel three inches below said there was no status to report. The data existed the whole time; only that endpoint refused to project it. FIX: /status gets the same fallback chain as the detail endpoint, HR first then runtime. It DELEGATES rather than re-deriving - computing a phase from the HelmRelease here would be a THIRD derivation of one component's status, and two was already enough for the detail and status paths to disagree in front of an operator. ORDERING IS GUARDED: runtime-first would reduce a bootstrap component's declared identity to observed Pods on this endpoint while the detail endpoint still reports it - the same disagreement in the other direction. No spec is invented (no CR means no spec.placement; the Topology tab derives real placement from live Pods via .../placement, #3982). 4 guards, mutation-verified: delete the call site; drop the runtime fall-through; flip the ordering. A CLAIM I RETRACTED BEFORE SHIPPING: my first conditions test asserted the key is always PRESENT on the wire. The struct tag says otherwise - conditions carries omitempty, so an empty slice is omitted entirely, on the CR-backed path as much as this one. Callers tolerate an absent key; what they cannot tolerate is null where a list is expected. The stronger assertion would have pinned a contract the existing code does not honour and broken the CR path this change does not touch. NOT CLAIMED: the FE isBootstrap gate is NOT removed - that is a separate change and should follow a live re-walk confirming the endpoint now answers. This row stays partial until then. Gates: go vet clean, full handler suite rc=0 254s; noted honestly, an immediately prior identical run reported FAIL at 253s with no capturable failure lines, so there is an unidentified flake in the suite and this is ONE clean run, not a verified-stable claim. ‖ hw292-2026-08-10 PROMOTED ⚠️ → ❌ — deploy-gated on the API half, but a REAL FRONT-END WALL remains that a roll cannot clear. #5836 makes `/status` answer, yet `TopologyTab.tsx:144` still disables the poll when `isBootstrap`, and `:1045-1047` HARD-RENDERS the literal `n/a — bootstrap component` string regardless of what the API returns. Both lines are still on main today. So the row needs the front-end un-gated, not merely the image rolled. ‖ PARTITION 2026-08-10: DEPLOY-GATED — the fix is MERGED and simply not running on this env. Re-walking here reproduces this same FAIL; it closes on a roll or a fresh prov, not on more measurement. ‖ hw293-2026-08-10 LIVE READ-ONLY WALK. Both regions were read through their OWN apiservers rather than through the console, so nothing here can be a transport artefact of the edge flapping. ❌ — grafana is NOT Running in both regions. Region-a: pod grafana-67dc9cfbd-vdrxz Running, 3/3 containers ready, 0 restarts; and GET /applications/grafana/status now answers phase=Ready with conditions Ready=True and Released=True ('Helm install succeeded for release grafana/grafana.v1 with chart bp-grafana@1.0.18'), so the #5836 + #5934 chain is working — the Status panel has a real status to render and no longer prints the bootstrap n/a string. Region-b: ZERO grafana pods exist, and HelmRelease flux-system/bp-grafana is False with the message dependency 'flux-system/bp-keycloak' is not ready. NAMED ROOT CAUSE, and it is a region-b data-plane fault rather than the symptom this clause names: region-b bp-keycloak failed post-install because keycloak-0 cannot mount its secret — kubelet reports MountVolume.SetUp failed for volume keycloak-secrets: secret keycloak-database-secret not found, x33 over 51m — and SEVEN further region-b HelmReleases are blocked behind it (bp-gitea, bp-grafana, bp-guacamole, bp-newapi, bp-oidc-gate, bp-powerdns-admin, bp-sso-bridge). ARITHMETIC CONTROL that this is the whole story and not a partial one: 65 region-b HelmReleases total minus the 8 wedged equals 57 ready, which is exactly the region-b figure the deployment record reports. EXPLICITLY NOT the 'cannot resolve write host' crashloop this clause names — grafana is absent in region-b, not crashlooping — recorded that way so the next walker does not go debugging a write-host resolution that is not happening. |

COVERAGE LIMIT: only the m tier exists on this estate, so 'at every tier' is verified for m and simply not instantiated for free or S — no Organization on those plans exists here to test. |
| 107 | placement | [#3642](https://github.com/openova-io/openova/issues/3642) | Deleting an Organization removes its vCluster StatefulSet — no orphaned vCluster survives the delete cascade. | hw293-2026-08-10 | ❌ | hw293-2026-08-10T17:38-17:44Z WALKED — ❌, and the failure is at the FIRST step of the cascade rather than in a late reaper. SUBJECT BUILT FOR THE ROW: `POST /api/v1/organizations` with plan_slug=m created Org `uat107vc` (id 576a7f40…), which the org-controller backed with a real dedicated vCluster — StatefulSet `uat107vc/vcluster` uid 297c4a97-0853-4e1b-9d25-e76dac386b76, created 17:38:30Z, readyReplicas 1, pod `vcluster-0` 1/1, plus bp-agenity / bp-keycloak / bp-keycloak-postgresql. MUTATION: `DELETE /api/v1/organizations/576a7f40-498f-4642-a11a-5a5f1cc8b067` → **204 No Content** at 17:41:41Z. AFTER, at 17:43:59Z — 138 seconds later: namespace `uat107vc` is Active and 30 SECONDS OLD (uid 517be74b…, creationTimestamp 17:43:29Z) and StatefulSet `uat107vc/vcluster` is present again with a DIFFERENT uid 95c1f3ee-63e8-4ea3-8267-01b57f96e21c created 17:43:33Z. The uid change is what makes this a measurement rather than a guess: the vCluster WAS torn down and then RE-CREATED, so an orphaned vCluster demonstrably survives the delete. ROOT CAUSE, exact, from the catalyst-api log at 17:38:40Z on the identical delete of the sibling Org uat107org: `org-tenant: Organization CR delete FAILED — finalizer cascade NOT triggered … err: organizations.orgs.openova.io "uat107org" is forbidden: User "system:serviceaccount:catalyst-system:catalyst-api-cutover-driver" cannot delete resource "organizations" in API group "orgs.openova.io" at the cluster scope`. Confirmed independently against the live cluster — `kubectl auth can-i delete organizations.orgs.openova.io --as=…catalyst-api-cutover-driver` → **no**, while `create` → **yes** — and confirmed in source on origin/main: `products/catalyst/chart/templates/clusterrole-cutover-driver.yaml:458` grants get/list/watch and `:715` grants create on `organizations`, and NO rule anywhere grants `delete`. So the #5426 CR-delete call that IS present in `handler/org_tenant_org_cr.go:161` runs, is refused, logs, and the handler still returns 204. Downstream: the Organization CR `uat107vc` kept deletionTimestamp EMPTY and resourceVersion frozen at 359196 with its `orgs.openova.io/tenant-networking` finalizer never firing; the per-Org Flux sources all survived Ready — GitRepository `catalyst-tenant-uat107vc` and Kustomizations `…-vcluster` / `…-host-apps` / `…-apps` — and it is those that re-authored the namespace and the vCluster. The API even keeps listing the Org: `GET /api/v1/organizations` returns it with state=deleted. VACUITY CONTROL: the same DELETE against the namespace-isolated Org uat107org behaved identically (204, CR untouched), so this is not a one-off race. Filed as live evidence on #5426.|

The GuacamoleConnection CRD + relay-controller the README used to describe do not exist (no Go type, no CRD manifest, no reconciler, no chart template). A seeded row also needs a WIRE fix, not just an INSERT: bp-k8s-ws-proxy rejects any upgrade without X-Catalyst-Timestamp + X-Catalyst-HMAC (core/cmd/k8s-ws-proxy/DESIGN.md:32-37, 401 in its failure table) and guacd's kubernetes protocol handler cannot emit them, so a naive INSERT would render a connection that 401s on click — passing this row while the feature stayed broken. Deliberately NOT seeded for that reason. Reset to unwalked because a new clause has never been walked; inheriting the old verdict would be fabrication. ‖ PARTITION 2026-08-10: NEEDS-CODE — the producer does not exist. This row is engineering work (the INSERT path plus the guacd-to-ws-proxy wire), not a walk; a re-walk cannot change it. ‖ hw293-2026-08-10 FIRST WALK OF THE REPLACEMENT CLAUSE, ON A FRESH PROV — PROVEN FAIL, measured against the table the clause names. Read-only psql on the live CNPG instance guacamole/guacamole-pg-1, database guacamole_db: SELECT count(*) FROM guacamole_connection returns 0, and SELECT count(*) FROM guacamole_connection_group returns 0. So the ALL CONNECTIONS list has nothing to render, on an env that is 82 minutes old and has never been touched by a walker. THE ROW'S OWN VACUITY GUARD IS WHAT THE DATABASE SHOWS: guacamole_entity holds exactly two rows — emrah.baysal@openova.io (USER) and sovereign-admins (USER_GROUP) — and guacamole_system_permission grants that group ADMINISTER plus CREATE_CONNECTION, CREATE_CONNECTION_GROUP, CREATE_SHARING_PROFILE, CREATE_USER, CREATE_USER_GROUP. That is the RIGHT to create a connection and not a connection, which the clause declares a FAIL, so the guard fires exactly as written. THE MISSING PRODUCER IS VISIBLE AS A RUNNING OBJECT, not only in source: CronJob guacamole-bp-guacamole-admin-enroll runs every minute and its latest Job completed 4s with the log '[jdbc-seed] db reachable / [jdbc-seed] enrolled 0 user(s) into sovereign-admins'. The only recurring writer the chart ships enrols IDENTITY. Nothing on this Sovereign creates a connection row. NOT A DEPLOY GAP: the platform tier here is healthy — guacamole-server 1/1, guacd 1/1, guacamole-pg-1 1/1, and the guacamole.hw293.omantel.biz HTTPRoute present via oidc-gate — so this is the producer being absent, not a wedged rollout. #5992 removed a fabricated embed URL and did not add an INSERT path; the state it left behind is the state measured here. DELIBERATELY NOT SEEDED BY THIS WALK: a bare INSERT would render a connection that 401s on click, because bp-k8s-ws-proxy requires X-Catalyst-Timestamp + X-Catalyst-HMAC on upgrade and guacd's kubernetes handler cannot emit them — passing the row while the feature stayed broken. The row needs the INSERT path AND that wire, and remains engineering work. |

The Jobs feed's leaf `task-syft-sbom` execution carries the IDENTICAL startedAt and reads `status: running, finishedAt: null`, and its parent group `reconcilers` therefore reads `running` — with **0 failed leaves out of 183**. THE DEFECT: the Day-2 scanner collapse never TERMINATES a failed execution, so a failed Job reads running forever and the group inherits the optimistic state. What still failed is one layer down, in the STORE bridge, and it reproduces the live symptom exactly. (A) jobs/reconciler_bridge.go seeded a cron leaf runs in LIST order and StartExecution stamps Job.Status=running on every allocation, so whichever run was processed LAST left the headline - an in-flight run listed after a failed one turned the leaf, and therefore the group, `running`. The ObsKindTask branch already re-asserted a resolved status with a comment naming this exact hazard; the ObsKindCron branch simply had no such line. (B) seedCronExecutionsLocked deduped by run name and left an already-recorded run UNTOUCHED, so a run first observed in flight was written running/FinishedAt=nil and never terminated when it later failed - verbatim the hw292 reading of `task-syft-sbom` carrying the failed Job startedAt while reporting `status: running, finishedAt: null`. Both are fixed with failing-first tests in reconciler_bridge_stale_run_164_test.go, each with a control that answers the other way (the task leaf passes unfixed, locating A in the cron branch; an in-flight run re-observed twice must STAY running, so the terminate path cannot fire on everything it re-sees). A first attempt at (A) re-asserted the producer status unconditionally and was caught by the pre-existing TestReconcilerBridge_FailingCron_RedWhileInstallGreen, because a cron leaf status is its latest RUN and not the producer - the shipped fix folds the runs through resolveRunHeadline instead. NOT FLIPPED GREEN: this is source work delivered against a named live symptom, and the row owes a re-walk on an env carrying it. ‖ hw293-2026-08-10 NOT MEASURABLE ON THE CURRENT ENV — verdict and walk stamp deliberately UNCHANGED; nothing was re-measured and no new claim is made. Recorded only so the next walker does not mistake an absence for the defect this row names. On hw293 there are ZERO cutover Job objects to form a group at all: bp-self-sovereign-cutover@0.1.177 never installed, `create: failed to create: Secret "sh.helm.release.v1.self-sovereign-cutover.v1" is invalid: data: Too long: must have at most 1048576 bytes`, first 13:04:38Z and last 13:17:56Z, #6004. THE DISTINCTION THAT MATTERS AND THAT THIS NOTE EXISTS TO PRESERVE: "no cutover group exists" is a DIFFERENT observation from "a group with a failed child reads a fake Succeeded", and collapsing the first into the second would charge a chart-size fault to the rollup code this row is actually about — a failure attributed to the wrong cause is worse than an unwalked row. Neither half was sampled in any case, since a group needs children to roll up. NOTE ON THE EDGE, corrected inside this pass: the console VIP is half-unhealthy — about half of all FRESH-TCP connections get envoy 503 `no healthy upstream` on EVERY path (20x each with curl --no-keepalive: / 11/20, /sovereign/jobs 10/20, /healthz 9/20, /sovereign/ 10/20), and a served connection answers /api/v1/whoami with 401, so the API is alive. An earlier single-shot reading in this same pass mistook that per-connection split for a per-path outage; one sample against a 50 percent split is a coin flip. It does not bear on this row either way. The store-side work recorded above still owes its re-walk on an env that carries a genuinely FAILED child, and hw293 is not that env. ‖ RE-TAGGED 2026-08-11: NEEDS-CODE -> DEPLOY-GATED — the rollup fix recorded above is ALREADY RUNNING here, and the remaining gate is a different fix that is also merged. The failed-run terminate work shipped as #5971 (`f420f7154`, 2026-08-10T10:32Z) and IS an ancestor of the deployed catalyst-api `70f6b07` (`/api/v1/version`), with `22ebec37d` returning non-ancestor from the same command as the control. What this env lacks is any cutover group at all — `bp-self-sovereign-cutover` cannot install because its Helm release Secret exceeds 1 MiB (#6004) — and THAT fix is `519d373cf` (2026-08-10T16:33Z), merged after this env fired at 12:23Z and not running here. So the row is gated on a roll carrying `519d373cf`, not on engineering; it then still needs a genuinely failed child to be discriminating. |

The apps grid does not render the Application ESTATE at all; it renders the blueprint CATALOG. AppsPage.tsx:116 builds its card list from `resolveApplications(store.selectedComponents)`, and applicationCatalog.ts:72 iterates BOOTSTRAP_KIT plus the selected component set — i.e. the wizard store's catalog. Application CRs never contribute card IDENTITY. They are layered in only as STATUS: AppsPage.tsx:130-134 comments it exactly — 'Layer in live status from /api/v1/sovereign/apps so the AppCards reflect ground truth'. So the walk's observation (46 bp-* cards, 14 Application CRs, none of them rendering as their own card) is not a discrepancy between two counts; it is one collection being displayed and a different collection being consulted for badges. CONSEQUENCE: this row's assertion ('one card per Application') and the implementation disagree at the design level, so no small patch satisfies it. Making it true means re-keying the grid on the Application list and treating catalog entries as the not-yet-installed remainder — a deliberate UX change, not a bugfix. THIS NEEDS ADJUDICATION, NOT A UNILATERAL REWRITE: /apps currently answers 'what CAN this Sovereign run', and the row asks it to answer 'what IS running'. Both are legitimate products; only the founder should choose. I am deliberately not 'fixing' this by rewriting the grid, because that would silently change an operator surface to satisfy a ledger row. Sibling: row 188 (catalyst-api likewise has no card) is the same cause. ‖ THE UI LEG IS FIXED IN SOURCE 2026-08-07 (#5827, PR pending; deploy-gated on hw292). This row's own evidence contained the contradiction and it took a source read to see it: GET .../applications/catalyst-api/placement answers 200 with a correct single Primary target (this row measured that), while GET .../applications/catalyst-api answers 404 - two endpoints on the SAME path prefix disagreeing about whether the component exists. Cause: catalyst-api is a Deployment rendered BY the bp-catalyst-platform HR, so it owns neither an Application CR nor a same-named HelmRelease and HandleApplicationGet's two lookups both miss; the placement endpoint does not care because it derives from LIVE PODS. So the API simultaneously knew the component and denied it existed depending on the suffix, and the console rendered 'App not found - the component catalyst-api is not part of this deployment' for something plainly running in front of the caller. That is exactly why this row's own subject could not be walked in a browser while its backend clause passed. FIX: a THIRD and last fallback, synthesiseAppFromRuntime, after the CR and the HR both miss. ONE IDENTITY PREDICATE, NOT TWO - existence is decided by podBelongsToComponent, the exact function the placement path uses; re-answering the same question a second way IS the defect, not an implementation detail of it. Readiness likewise reuses the existing podIsReady (I wrote one, found the identical function in dashboard.go, deleted mine). FABRICATES NOTHING: no CR means no uid/blueprint/version/parameters/environmentRef, all left empty rather than guessed; runtimeDerived:true marks the whole answer as an OBSERVATION so a consumer can tell 'declares nothing' from 'declaration not fetched' without inferring it from which fields are blank. Phase is strict - Ready requires EVERY matched Pod ready, because rounding one degraded replica of three up to Ready is how a status panel starts lying. ORDERING IS LOAD-BEARING and guarded: the runtime path is LAST, because running it before the HR lookup would strip every bootstrap component's declared blueprint down to the bare observed shape. 4 guards, mutation-verified. ONE CAUGHT ITSELF and is worth recording: the first omitempty mutation SILENTLY DID NOT APPLY - gofmt had realigned the struct field so the replace matched nothing - and the test 'passed'. A mutation that does not apply is not a passing test, it is an unexecuted mutation. Redone against the real text; it then went red as intended. Gates: go vet clean, full handler suite 254s green. NOT FIXED HERE: this does not give bootstrap components Application CRs. Rows 19, 67 and 69 all trace to that same structural absence and are untouched. ‖ NO-PARTIALS DISPOSITION 2026-08-10 — RECLASSIFIED ⚠️ -> ❌. Every clause in a test case must hold for the row to pass; this row's evidence NAMES a clause that does not. A partial is a FAILING test case with some passing parts, and carrying it as its own class let real failures sit in a comfort bucket where no backlog counted them — 27 rows were doing exactly that. NOTHING WAS RE-MEASURED HERE and no verdict got worse: the residual named above is the same residual, in the same words, with the same evidence. Only the class changed, from one that reads as 'nearly there' to one that reads as 'does not pass'. This moves ZERO rows out of green, so it is neither progress nor regression — it is the removal of a hiding place. The row closes when the named residual closes. ‖ PARTITION 2026-08-10: DEPLOY-GATED — the fix is MERGED and simply not running on this env. Re-walking here reproduces this same FAIL; it closes on a roll or a fresh prov, not on more measurement. ‖ hw293-2026-08-10 LIVE READ-ONLY WALK. ❌ ON A VACUITY GROUND, and the control is the finding rather than a footnote to it. The literal clause does hold: catalyst-api genuinely IS single-region (one pod, namespace catalyst-system, region-a only; region-b has none), and GET /applications/catalyst-api/placement returns exactly one target, hw-me-east-215-a-rtz-prod role=Primary, with derivedFromRuntime=true and no false multi-region. But this surface cannot report anything else, so the right answer here is not evidence that it was correctly derived. THE CONTROL, taken from each region's OWN apiserver rather than from the console: bp-alloy runs 6 pods in region-a and 6 in region-b, powerdns 7 and 5, cilium 14 and 14, openova-flow 10 and 9 — and the same placement endpoint returns the identical single region-a Primary for alloy and for powerdns, and targets=0 for cilium and for openova-flow. Singleton is therefore the only value this endpoint produces for any component on this Sovereign. ROOT CAUSE, measured: the Sovereign's catalyst-api holds an EMPTY /var/lib/catalyst/kubeconfigs directory — the exact path its own CATALYST_K8SCACHE_KUBECONFIGS_DIR env var names — and every k8scache 'informer synced' log line carries cluster=a0077ba47e3720e5, one cluster, never a second. The console has no credential for region-b, whose apiserver is otherwise entirely healthy and answers 6 nodes to a direct query. So the live defect is the MIRROR of the one this row guards against: not false multi-region, but FALSE SINGLETON on genuinely two-region components. A ✅ here would tell a future reader this surface discriminates single-region apps correctly, which is the opposite of what was measured. CODE/DELIVERY defect on a served connection — not transport, and not region-b degradation, since region-b itself is reachable and healthy. |

ZERO 'steady-state heal' lines appear anywhere in 180 minutes of mothership catalyst-api log. This is precisely the gap the code comment at internal/handler/deployment_handover_export.go:440 anticipates — a missed one-shot handover-export window leaves the chroot's kubeconfig dir empty forever and #3991/#4001 'never address DELIVERY'. UPDATE TO WHAT #6015 RECORDED: that issue observed the directory EMPTY at 13:08; by 15:09 the region-a file had self-registered while the secondary still had not arrived, so the gap is specifically the SECONDARY leg and not first-write. COUPLING WORTH NAMING: the failed status originates in the #6004 cutover component, so one over-size Helm release Secret is also what suppresses secondary-kubeconfig delivery and, through it, multi-region topology. ‖ PARTITION 2026-08-11: DEPLOY-GATED — merged 22ebec37d (PR #6024, Refs #6015), NOT an ancestor of the running catalyst-api sha 70f6b07aa. this row names DELIVERY and the fix is the delivery seam: reforwardSecondaryKubeconfigsToChild had one caller behind the ready branch, and 22ebec37d re-homes it in a level-triggered loop re-armed on every startup restore. |

IDENTITY, stated precisely rather than glossed: the bearer is a real passwordless-PIN console session (RS256, realm_access.roles `[catalyst-admin]`), and the MCP resolves it to `context: sovereign` / `tier: owner` with `sovereign_admin: false` — the literal `sovereign-admin` TIER needs a `sovereign-admins` group or `role: sovereign-admin` claim that the PIN mint does not emit here, and the row's own earlier stamp records that a handover-minted sovereign-admin JWT is rejected upstream by catalyst-api. So what is proven is the SOVEREIGN-CONTEXT unfiltered listing the clause is about, from the only bearer this Sovereign accepts end-to-end. RE-MEASURED ACROSS INDEPENDENT CONNECTIONS: 3/3 on fresh TCP pinned to the region-A node, plus 4/6 on the public EIP URL (the other 2 returned envoy `no healthy upstream` with no upstream contacted — the region-B split, not an MCP answer); every one of the 7 answers that reached the MCP returned the same total 5 and the same two namespaces. |

| 212 | mcp | [#3988](https://github.com/openova-io/openova/issues/3988) | MCP **Org-scoped** token → `list_applications` returns ONLY that Org's apps (RBAC parity). | hw293-2026-08-10 | ❌ | hw293-2026-08-10 LIVE MCP WALK — ❌, and the row cannot even reach its own assertion: an Org-scoped bearer is REJECTED AT THE MCP'S AUTH LAYER, so `list_applications` never runs under Org scope. An Org-scoped session was obtained the real way — passwordless PIN against the per-Org console host console.hw293walkone.omani.homes, which catalyst-api's #4110 host-trust path forces Org-scoped; the minted `catalyst_session` decodes to `{tier: org-admin, org_id: hw293walkone, realm_access.roles: [org-admin]}`, alg RS256. Presented to https://mcp.hw293.omantel.biz/mcp it returns **`-32001 unauthenticated — identity: bearer rejected: token signature is invalid: crypto/rsa: verification error`, 3/3**, for `whoami` and for `list_applications` alike. TWO CONTROLS, both run in the same minutes, which is what makes this a finding rather than a bad token: (1) the byte-identical 839-char string is ACCEPTED by catalyst-api itself — `GET /api/v1/whoami` with it as a Bearer and no cookie returns HTTP 200 `{"tier":"org-admin","orgScoped":true,"org":"hw293walkone"}`; (2) the SOVEREIGN-scoped session minted by the SAME catalyst-api one minute earlier is accepted by the SAME MCP endpoint 3/3, interleaved probe-for-probe with the failing Org one (SOV ok / ORG err, three times). So the transport is fine, the token is intact and correctly signed for catalyst-api, and only the Org-scoped one fails at the MCP. SURFACE TO LOOK AT, NAMED NOT ASSERTED: the MCP verifies against a SINGLE pinned RS256 public key — `NewRS256Resolver(pub, pinnedCtx)` in products/openova-mcp/internal/identity/identity.go:213, whose own comment records that "the full per-realm JWKS cache remains the §4.2 follow-up" (#3988). That is where a key/issuer mismatch between the sovereign-console mint and the Org-console mint would land; I did NOT confirm which key each token carries, and both headers are bare `{alg: RS256, typ: JWT}` with no `kid` to discriminate. WHAT THIS ROW STILL NEEDS: the Org bearer accepted, then a count assertion that the returned set is exactly hw293walkone's 3 Applications (uat174-wp, uat50-ahs-pg, uat224-openclaw) and none of hw293walktwo's 4. |

### Governance train — items 187-215 (added 2026-06-21)

These extend the canonical 186-case walk with this session's governance work. **Status is deliberately conservative** (no premature ✅): `⏳` = built/merged in code but **NOT yet validated on the durable live console** (the agents' temporary image rolls were Flux-reverted, so they are not yet what the operator sees — each needs a real end-user walk after the durable roll); `◑` = mechanism proven (e.g. against live HCS) but full in-cluster proof lands on a fresh prov; `❌` = not built yet (fix in flight) or genuinely requires a clean re-prov. A row flips `✅` only after an operator end-user walk on the durable console with pasted evidence, per the TRUST-ledger discipline.

Both names resolve to the SINGLE VIP 212.72.24.49, so this is not DNS round-robin between regions but per-connection load-balancing across an upstream set in which about half the endpoints are unhealthy. WHAT WAS NOT WALKED, and why: no valid voucher exists on this env, and completing a redemption would CREATE an Organization, which this read-only walk is forbidden to do — so the voucher→Launch leg is unwalked, as it was on hw292. THE OUTCOME HALF IS INDEPENDENTLY TRUE ON THIS ENV BUT WAS NOT REACHED VIA A VOUCHER: two customer Orgs already exist (hw293walkone on omani.homes, hw293walktwo on omani.trade), both state=done, boundary_phase=Ready, all six provisioning steps done (vcluster, bp_charts, dns, certs, keycloak_clients, registry), kind=customer, tier=org, billing real, isolation vcluster; and the fleet endpoint lists a live vcluster app (blueprint vcluster 0.33.4) status=Ready in each Org namespace — so 'ACTIVE backed by live vcluster' is real rather than fake-green. (1) curl https://wordpress.hw293walkone.omani.homes/ → 302 → /wp-admin/install.php → 200, title 'WordPress › Installation'; identically for https://wordpress.hw293walktwo.omani.trade/. (2) mysql pods mysql-...-x-hw293walkone-x-vcluster and mysql-...-x-hw293walktwo-x-vcluster are both QoS=Guaranteed and Running. (3) HTTPRoute app-wordpress-hostroute exists in each Org namespace, parents Gateway kube-system/cilium-gateway-console, Accepted=True and ResolvedRefs=True. (4) both hosts resolve to 212.72.24.49, the consoleLoadBalancerIP, NOT the primary ELB 212.72.24.74. WHAT FAILS: 12 FRESH TCP connections to wordpress.hw293walkone.omani.homes gave 6 x 302 and 6 x TLS connection-reset-by-peer — half of a paying customer's first loads do not complete a handshake. MECHANISM, read from both regions' live objects: the console Gateway in region A declares TEN listeners including console-https-hw293walkone / console-http-hw293walkone / console-https-hw293walktwo / console-http-hw293walktwo, while the SAME Gateway in region B declares only SIX — the four per-Org listeners were never written there — and region B holds ZERO Org HTTPRoutes. Both regions sit behind the one EIP, so the half that lands on B has no SNI match and resets. CONTROL that isolates it to the missing listener rather than to the EIP: console.hw293.omantel.biz, whose listener DOES exist in both regions, completes TLS on the same EIP and fails differently — 6 of 12 return HTTP 503 'no healthy upstream'. Listener present → 503; listener absent → reset. THIS IS NOT DEPLOY-GATED, AND THAT IS THE NEW FINDING. The #5930 fan-out (4ca4c34b4, 'per-Org console listeners span every region the ELB pool does') IS an ancestor of the deployed catalyst-api 70f6b07, checked with merge-base. It runs and reports success anyway: catalyst-api logs, every 5 minutes, 'org-console-tls: ensured per-Org console TLS trio — listener pair admitted in every region' with the fan-out's own field regions="host" — ONE pseudo-region, never me-east-215-b. Cause, traced to the seam: orgConsoleTLSPoolRegions() (org_console_tls.go:389) builds the pool region set from the on-disk secondary kubeconfigs under CATALYST_KUBECONFIGS_DIR, and inside the Sovereign's own catalyst-api pod /var/lib/catalyst/kubeconfigs is EMPTY — the mothership holds both regions' kubeconfigs, the Sovereign holds neither. len(keys)==0 returns nil, so the pool is empty, so `unreached` stays empty too and the loud 'NO client could be built for it' error never fires. Then the admission read-back loops over the SAME truncated target list, which is why it can report 'admitted in every region' having checked one: a guard iterating the set it is meant to validate cannot detect a set that lost a member. Config, for the record: ConfigMap catalyst-system/sovereign-fqdn declares configuredRegions hw-me-east-215-a-rtz-prod,hw-me-east-215-b-rtz-prod and replicaRegion hw-me-east-215-b-rtz-prod, so the second region is DECLARED and simply unreachable to this writer. Same defect family as #5511/#5246, reproduced with the remedy running. ‖ RE-TAGGED 2026-08-11: NEEDS-CODE -> DEPLOY-GATED — the seam this walk traced now HAS a merged fix, and it is in the same file. The walk was right that #5930 was merged-and-deployed-and-still-failing, and it located the cause precisely at `orgConsoleTLSPoolRegions` in `org_console_tls.go` reading an empty kubeconfigs dir. #6024 (`22ebec37d`, 2026-08-10T16:00:11Z) is that repair: it touches `products/catalyst/bootstrap/api/internal/handler/org_console_tls.go`, moves secondary-kubeconfig delivery into a level-triggered `runSecondaryKubeconfigDelivery` reached for EVERY multi-region deployment whatever Phase 1 concluded (hw293's Phase 1 latched failed on one HelmRelease, which is what starved both previous producers), and makes placement refuse to answer from a cache narrower than the declared region set — closing the guard-iterates-its-own-truncated-set defect this cell describes. It is an ancestor of `origin/main` and NOT an ancestor of the deployed `70f6b07`. Closes on a roll.

SECOND SHAPE, same answer: the legacy singular-region body with no regions array returns the same 400 reading 0 region(s).
